/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:88:**Important:** on a high-core-count machine (128+ cores) `default_jobs()` computes `min(nproc, /dev/shm_free/256MB)` which can reach 128+. Running that many vsim Verilator processes simultaneously saturates CPU and causes timeouts. Use `JOBS=8` (or lower) as a practical cap: `JOBS=8 bash scripts/run_e2e.sh`.
/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:90:`scripts/run_e2e.sh` exits 0 only when all tests pass. Logs go to a `mktemp` directory under `/tmp`; path is printed at the start of each run. Each test's full output lands in `$LOG_DIR/$mode--$name.log`.
/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:92:**Manual driver tests**: `tests/andes_sim/test_andes_sim_*.sh` cover the C++ driver's feature contracts. They run manually during driver development, not wired into `scripts/run_e2e.sh`. Each script is self-contained (sources `setup.sh` artifacts, runs one subcommand, asserts on stdout). The same convention applies to `tests/plugin/test_plugin_*.sh` (QEMU-plugin-only smoke tests).
/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:109:**Privilege (L6)**: the handoff carries the real privilege level. The plugin drains the gdbstub `priv` register, `ResumeDriver` read-modify-writes `dcsr.prv` before `dret` (and verifies the write took -- an M-mode-only RTL like the default `ax45mpv_premium`, `NDS_NUM_PRIVILEGE_LEVELS=1`, WARL-pins the field, and the resume aborts with a pick-an-S/U-capable-engine error), and `StateDrain` reads `dcsr.prv` back at the exit halt. For S/U handoffs the producer's PMP block (64 `pmpaddr` + the 8 even `pmpcfg`) is also mirrored into the hart, because with PMP implemented and no matching entry every S/U access faults; M-mode handoffs keep PMP pass-through-only (L2). The `rt_priv_smode`/`rt_priv_umode` fixtures run on the S/U-capable `ax66_makatau` engine paired with QEMU `-cpu andes-ax66` (see `scripts/run_e2e.sh`); the ROI itself must still be trap-free, single-privilege, and paging-off (S-mode CSRs are not transported).
/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:119:`./andesim run --mode hybrid --trigger marker|pc` is the unified orchestrator; `tests/e2e.sh` drives marker/pc internally. The run yields a cycle figure: window cycles = the vsim `mcycle` delta (`hybrid_state_v1::mcycle`).
/home/nick/work/andesim/CLAUDE.md:131:**Memory-content sidecars (NOT in `hybrid_state_v1`)**: ILM/DLM and HVM content travels OUTSIDE the state struct, as raw binary sidecar files next to each state file. The plugin (args `ilm_size`/`dlm_size`/`hvm_size` in bytes; bases default `0xA0000000`/`0xA0200000`/`0x90000000`) drains `<state>.ilm/.dlm/.hvm` through the slave-port/subordinate apertures; the vsim leg backdoor-loads them (`--ilm/--dlm/--hvm` -- zero-sim-cycle Verilator hierarchical-reference pokes into the RTL SRAMs, NOT the DM Program Buffer); the exit drain dumps the read-duals next to the handoff-out state. Armed by the engine's `--describe` sizes (`ilm_size`/`dlm_size`/`hvm_size`, bytes; 0 = no memory or wrapper lacks the set_*/get_* backdoor pair -- capability-gated so a config-has-it/wrapper-can't engine arms nothing). These QEMU memories are per-CPU overlays (LM) or standalone RAM (HVM), NOT backed by the shared mmap, so the sidecars are the only road their content travels; QEMU's raw geometry must be synced (`-readconfig` on the driver path, `-global` in tests/e2e.sh -- HVM's property is `hvm_size_pow_2`, log2) because QEMU's compiled-in defaults (2 MB ILM at 0x0, 1-BYTE HVM) are wrong for every engine.

Always readable regardless of `mcounteren`. |\n| Function | A set CY/IR/HPM3~6 bit stops that counter from incrementing while in S-mode |\n\nTable 13 cross-check (consolidated existence table): `mcountermask_s`,\n`scountermask_m/s/u`, `scounterinten`, `scounterovf`, `scountinhibit`,\n`shpmevent3~6` all share the condition\n`(mmsc_cfg.PMNDS==1) & (misa[18]==1) // supervisor`.\n\nSection 9.6.1 (`mcounterwen`) explicitly lists `scountermask_s` among the\nregisters whose write behavior `mcounterwen` controls -- so the existing\nrestore-ordering prerequisite (write-enable before the masks) applies to the\nalias exactly as it does to 0x7D2.\n\n## 2. Option A (recommended): no wire change\n\nBecause 0x9D2 and 0x7D2 are one register, adding 0x9D2 to the wire would:\n\n- transport the same 64-bit value twice;\n- restore it twice (each DM op costs ~200-900 simulated cycles) and sweep it\n  twice at drain;\n- verify-compare a guaranteed-duplicate field.\n\nRequired work: none on the wire. Optional hardening / documentation:\n\n1. Keep the matrix row `EXCL_MODE`. If the team wants the alias fact recorded\n   machine-readably, introduce an `EXCL_ALIAS` disposition in\n   `scripts/classify_csr_coverage.py` (OVERRIDE entry for `scountermask_s`,\n   plus the legend in the docstring and `docs/csr_coverage.ipynb`) and\n   regenerate `csr_coverage.csv` through the classifier (never by hand). Behavioral gotchas (apply to both options)\n\na) **mcounterwen write-gating**: S-alias bits are read-only where the matching\n   `mcounterwen` bit is 0. Any fixture writing through 0x9D2 must set\n   `mcounterwen` first or the write silently drops. The transport itself is\n   safe: it restores through the M-primary 0x7D2 (plain M-writable), and the\n   X-macro order already restores `mcounterwen` (0x7CE, list position 13)\n   before the masks (position 15).\nb) **QEMU model divergence**: QEMU's `write_scounter` writes the FULL masked\n   value when `mcounterwen == 0` and applies per-bit gating only when\n   `wen != 0` -- inverted from the spec's read-only-when-0 rule. A fixture\n   that writes 0x9D2 with wen=0 would take effect in QEMU but not in the RTL,\n   producing a false `--verify` diff. Designing the fixture to set wen bits\n   first (gotcha (a)) sidesteps the divergence.\nc) **Engine scope**: on ax45mpv_premium (M-only) the CSR does not exist; the\n   SMODE gate keeps it out of restore/drain/verify -- do not \"test\" this CSR\n   there. QEMU-exposes != vsim-implements (the known premium gotcha).\nd) **Measurement semantics**: if a future S-mode ROI runs with\n   scountermask_s.CY set, mcycle stops counting in S-mode -- the transported\n   mask value directly shapes the cycle figure. On a FRESHLY CONFIGURED\nengine the concrete cause is almost always one of two things, and both\nfire during the reset-baseline capture that runs at the very start of\nresume:\n\n1. **The batched CSR sweep hit a caps over-claimed CSR.** The caps map\n   (`andes_caps.hpp` + `andes_caps_config.hpp`) claims the new engine\n   implements a CSR its RTL does not have. The drain-side sweep executes\n   `csrr` on it in the Program Buffer, the hart raises illegal\n   instruction, and the whole batch aborts. This is the expected failure\n   mode of a new engine config: the caps gates were tuned for the old\n   engines and the new config.inc turns a different set of `_NDS_*`\n   knobs on.\n\n2. **mstatus.FS/VS is Off (or WARL-pinned off) for the FPR/V sweeps.**\n   The capture and resume enable FS/VS before any `fld`/`fsd`/`vsetvl`/\n   `vle8.v`/`vse8.v` Program-Buffer execution. If the new engine has no\n   F/D or no V extension at all (while the QEMU leg drained FP/V state\n   and set `HYBRID_FLAG_FP`/`HYBRID_FLAG_V`), the mstatus enable write\n   pins FS/VS to 0 and the very first FP/V instruction in the Program\n   Buffer raises illegal instruction -> cmderr=3.\n\nWhy \"at resume\" on a fresh engine specifically: the reset-baseline cache\nkey includes the sim binary's mtime and size\n(`vsim src/hybrid/reset_baseline.hpp:26-35`), so a freshly built engine\nALWAYS misses the cache. On a miss, `apply_resume_from_qemu` captures the\nbaseline first (`vsim src/simulator.hpp:217-238`): it halts the freshly\nreset hart and runs a FULL drain with the CSR sweep enabled\n(`capture_reset_baseline`, `src/simulator.hpp:349-393`,\n`drain.set_csr_sweep(true)` at line 358). That sweep rides the batched\n`csrr`+`sd` Program Buffer path over the COMPLETE caps-supported CSR\nlist -- so one over-claimed CSR faults the batch before the restore even\nstarts. The exception escapes to the top-level catch in\n`vsim src/main.cpp:544`, which prints the `[critical] fatal exception:`\nline you saw.\n\nThe raw message string comes from `exec_progbuf` in\n`vsim src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp:201-205`. Deliberate design: no\ncaller may silently continue on a PB fault -- the hart would be left\npartially restored/drained, which is worse than aborting.\n\n## Step 0: read the error message you actually have\n\nThe CSR sweep bisects its own faults. Do not trust QEMU: its gdbstub advertises CSRs the vsim RTL\ndoes not implement (QEMU models a fuller core).\n\n```sh\n# the BUILD directory copy, not the external/ source tree\nless $VSIM_BIN_DIR/V<engine>/obj_sc/config.inc\n$VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<engine> --describe\n```\n\nFrom config.inc, note the `_NDS_*` knobs relevant to the fault:\n- privilege: `NDS_NUM_PRIVILEGE_LEVELS` (1 = M-only, 2 = M+U, 3 = M+S+U)\n- features: DSP / CODENSE / POWERBRAKE / STACKSAFE / ECC / PMA / ILM /\n  DLM / PERF_MONITOR / TRIGGER / CLIC / SMSTATEEN / CCTL / PPI / FIO ...\n- extensions: does the RTL have F/D? What the message means\n\nThe thrower is `exec_progbuf()` in\n`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp` (line 201-205).\nIt writes a short instruction sequence into the RISC-V Debug Module **Program\nBuffer**, tells the halted hart to execute it, then reads back\n`abstractcs.cmderr`. The exception escapes to the `catch` in\n`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/main.cpp:543` which prints the\n`[critical] fatal exception:` line and exits 1 (so the driver then reports a\n\"plan step 1: expected exit 200, got 1\" style error).\n\n`cmderr` is the RISC-V Debug Spec abstract-command error code:\n\n| cmderr | meaning |\n|--------|---------|\n| 1 | busy |\n| 2 | not supported |\n| 3 | **exception - the hart took an architectural exception while executing the injected instructions** |\n| 4 | halt/resume |\n| 5 | bus error |\n\nSo cmderr=3 means: one of the instructions the resume flow injected into the\nProgram Buffer **trapped inside the hart**. In this flow only two trap classes\noccur in practice:\n\n1. **Illegal instruction** - the instruction touches an extension or CSR the\n   engine's RTL does not implement (no FPU -> `fld`/`fsd` trap; no V ->\n   `vsetvl`/`vle8.v`/`vse8.v` trap; `csrrw` to an unimplemented CSR traps), or\n   the extension is off in `mstatus` (FS/VS = Off, which on a core *without*\n   the extension is WARL-pinned to Off, so the enable write silently no-ops).\n2. **Load/store access fault** - the sweep's `ld`/`sd`/`fld`/`fsd`/\n   `vle8.v`/`vse8.v` addresses the shared-mmap scratch page at a guest\n   physical address the engine's memory decode does not map.\n\n**Important discriminator built into the code:** the Andes/M-ext CSR sweep\n(`csr_sweep()` in the same header) catches a faulting batch, bisects it one\nCSR at a time, and rethrows a *named* error:\n`csr_sweep: CSR 0x... raised cmderr -- the caps map over-claims a CSR ...`.\nTherefore, if you see the **raw** `exec_progbuf: PB execution raised cmderr=3`\nmessage, the fault is **NOT** in the Andes/M-ext CSR list. It is one of the\nfixed Program-Buffer sequences: the GPR/FPR block sweeps, the V data sweep,\nthe Tier-B `vsetvl`, or a single `csrrw` fallback.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Where it can throw, in run order\n\nA first hybrid run on a fresh engine executes, inside the one vsim process,\nin this order (`Simulator::apply_resume_from_qemu`,\n`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/simulator.hpp:208`):\n\n**Phase 0 - LG3 reset-baseline capture** (runs *before* resume; a freshly\nbuilt engine binary always misses the `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/andesim` cache, so this\nalways runs on the first hybrid run of a new build):\n\n| PB sequence | instructions | traps when |\n|---|---|---|\n| GPR block store | `sd x1..x30, off(x31)` | scratch page not mapped/writable |\n| FPR block store (if state FLAG_FP) | `fsd f0..f31, off(x5)` | **engine has no FPU** (FS pinned Off -> illegal insn), or scratch fault |\n| Andes+M-ext CSR sweep | `csrr` + `sd` per CSR | caps over-claim - but this one throws the NAMED `csr_sweep: CSR 0x...` error, not the raw one |\n| V dump sweep (if FLAG_V) | `vsetvl` then `vse8.v v0..v24` | **engine has no V** (illegal insn), or scratch fault |\n\n**Phase 1 - `ResumeDriver::resume()`**\n(`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/resume_driver.hpp:146`):\n\n| step | PB sequence | traps when |\n|---|---|---|\n| FPR reload (FLAG_FP) | `fld f0..f31, off(x5)` | no FPU / scratch fault |\n| fcsr, vstart, vxsat, vxrm, vcsr, mcycle, minstret | abstract write first; on abstract cmderr, PB fallback `csrrw x0, csr, x5` | the CSR does not exist on this engine (FP/V CSRs on a no-FP/no-V core) |\n| V reload (FLAG_V) | `vsetvl` + `vle8.v v0..v24` | no V / scratch fault |\n| Tier-B vtype+vl | `vsetvl x0, x5, x6` | no V |\n| GPR reload | `ld x1..x31, off(x31)` | scratch fault |\n| Andes+M-ext restore | abstract tolerant writes only | never throws (clears cmderr per CSR) |\n\nNote: both phases print the identical `[critical]` line, and the andesim\ndriver *hides* vsim's `[info]` log lines (commit b86d168), so from the driver\nyou cannot tell which phase died. Section 4 shows how to tell.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Root causes on a freshly configured engine, ranked\n\n### RC1 (by far the most common): QEMU fast leg models MORE ISA than the new engine\n\nThe QEMU plugin (`/home/nick/work/andesim/qemu_plugin/hybrid_handoff.c`,\n`drain_state_into`) sets `HYBRID_FLAG_FP` / `HYBRID_FLAG_V` when the QEMU\n*CPU model* advertises the FPU / V registers - **regardless of whether the\nworkload used them**. So even a pure scalar ELF drains FP + V state. The\nresume then executes `fld` / `vsetvl` / `vle8.v` on an engine whose RTL has no\nFPU / no V -> illegal instruction -> cmderr=3.\n\nThe framework's ISA-sync mechanism is: the driver probes the engine with\n`sim_<cfg> --print-qemu-config` and passes the result to QEMU via\n`-readconfig` (`/home/nick/work/andesim/driver/run_plan.cpp:248-255,184-187`).\nThree concrete ways a *fresh* engine breaks this chain:\n\n1. **Empty qemu.cfg.** The cfg is generated at engine-build time by\n   `external/cpu_config_to_march_converter/scripts/config_inc_to_qemu_cfg.py`\n   (`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/cmake/VerilatorHelpers.cmake:148-164`). If\n   that submodule was not initialized when you configured the new engine,\n   cmake embeds an **empty** qemu.cfg (\"No CPU_CONFIG_QEMU_CONVERTER\"), the\n   driver's probe returns empty, and the fast leg silently falls back to the\n   pinned default `-cpu andes-ax45mpv,vlen=<engine vlen|512>` - full FP + V.\n\n2. **Driver-name family mismatch.** qemu.cfg's `[global]` blocks target\n   `driver = \"andes-<family>-riscv-cpu\"` where `<family>` is the engine name\n   up to the first `_` (`ax45mpv_premium -> ax45mpv`,\n   VerilatorHelpers.cmake:147). But the hybrid fast leg **hardcodes**\n   `-cpu andes-ax45mpv,vlen={vlen}`\n   (`/home/nick/work/andesim/driver/engine_registry.cpp:52-53`). For any\n   engine whose family is not `ax45mpv` (e.g. a fresh `d25`, `ax66_...`,\n   `nx45v_...`), the globals name a device class QEMU never instantiates and\n   are **silently ignored** - QEMU still runs full ax45mpv ISA.\n\n3. **The converter is \"on\"-only.** `config_inc_to_qemu_cfg.py`\n   (`_collect_isa_props`) emits `value = \"on\"` for extensions present in the\n   config; it never emits `off` for absent ones (only `zcd` is ever forced\n   off). An extension that is ON by default in the base QEMU model but absent\n   in your engine config is never disabled.\n\nNet effect: state file flags claim FP/V/CSRs the engine lacks -> first PB\ninstruction touching them traps.\n\n### RC2: scratch-page access fault (memory map mismatch)\n\nEvery block sweep moves data through the V-scratch region: the TOP page of the\nshared mmap, GPA = `mmap_base + physical_mmap_bytes(mem_size) - 4096`\n(`v_scratch_gpa`, `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/v_regfile_xfer.hpp`).\nThe driver passes `--shared-mem-base 0x0` and\n`--shared-mem-size = --mem-size + one page` (default 128 MiB + 4 KiB), and\nvsim maps that window as its only RAM (`main.cpp:389-397`). If the fresh\nengine's decode does not cover that window - an ILM/DLM whose base overlaps\nit, a PMA reset configuration that rejects it, or a narrower physical address\ndecode - the first `sd`/`ld`/`fld`/`vle8.v` to scratch takes an access fault\n-> cmderr=3.\n\n### RC3: caps-map over-claim (different signature - listed to rule it OUT)\n\n`andes_caps_from_config()`\n(`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/andes_caps_config.hpp`) derives the\ntransported-CSR set from the `_NDS_*` macros in the engine's **built**\n`config.inc` (memory note: the built engine's\n`build/V<cfg>/obj_sc/config.inc` is the ground truth, which can differ from\nthe `external/` source tree). If a fresh config spells a feature macro\ndifferently, or a CSR falls into `andes_gate_for()`'s `ALWAYS` default, the\nsweep transports a CSR the hart lacks. But that fails with the **named**\n`csr_sweep: CSR 0x...` error (drain/baseline-capture side) or, in the known\nworst case, a DMI **hang / \"timed out\"** (documented for `ucode`/`mecc_code`\non ax45mpv_premium in `andes_caps.hpp`'s header comment). A raw\n`exec_progbuf ... cmderr=3` is not this; check RC1/RC2 first.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Step-by-step diagnosis\n\nAll commands are read-only probes plus manual re-runs of the failing legs.\n\n**Step 1 - get the exact leg commands.**\n\n```sh\ncd /home/nick/work/andesim\n./andesim run --mode hybrid --engine verilator:<cfg> --trigger marker --dry-run <elf>\n```\n\nInspect the fast-leg argv:\n- Is `-readconfig <workdir>/qemu.cfg` present at all? Missing => RC1(1):\n  the engine's `--print-qemu-config` probe returned nothing.\n- What `-cpu` is pinned? (`andes-ax45mpv,vlen=...` today.)\n\n**Step 2 - interrogate the engine binary** (the built binary is the truth,\nnot the `external/` tree):\n\n```sh\nSIM=$VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<cfg>          # from config.env\n$SIM --describe                      # vlen/xlen + \"andes_csrs\" the caps gate claims\n$SIM --print-qemu-config             # MUST be non-empty; note the driver= family\n$SIM --print-config | grep -E 'NDS_(RVV|FPU|VLEN|ELEN|ILM|DLM|PMA|NUM_PRIVILEGE)'\n$SIM --print-memmap                  # RAM window the decode actually maps\n```\n\nDecision points:\n- `--print-qemu-config` empty -> RC1(1). Fix 1.\n- `driver = \"andes-<family>-riscv-cpu\"` family != the `-cpu` family in the\n  dry-run fast leg -> RC1(2). Fix 2.\n- config.inc has no `NDS_RVV_SUPPORT` / no FPU, but the base `-cpu` model has\n  V/FP on by default -> RC1(3). Fix 2/3.\n- `--print-memmap` does not cover `[0x0, mem_size + 4096)` or shows ILM/DLM\n  inside that window -> RC2. Fix 4.\n\n**Step 3 - confirm what the state file claims.** Run the dry-run's QEMU\nfast-leg command by hand (the driver deletes its workdir, so use your own\npaths for `outfile=` and the mmap), then read the flags word:\n\n```sh\nxxd -l 12 state-0.bin     # bytes 0-3 magic 'AHYB', 4-7 version, 8-11 flags\n```\n\nFlags (`/home/nick/work/andesim/include/hybrid/state_abi.h`): bit0 RV64,\nbit1 FP, bit2 V, bit3 ANDES, bit4 M_EXT. `flags = 0x1f` on a scalar workload\nwith a scalar engine confirms RC1 (QEMU over-advertised). A python decoder\nexists at `/home/nick/work/andesim/tools/orchestrator/abi.py`.\n\n**Step 4 - pinpoint the throwing phase.** Run the vsim accurate-leg command\nby hand (from the dry run) with two additions:\n\n```sh\n$SIM <elf> --resume-from-qemu state-0.bin ... --log-level 1\n```\n\n- No `hybrid: reset baseline ...` info line before the crash -> died in the\n  LG3 **baseline-capture drain** (phase 0). (The driver hides `[info]` lines;\n  running by hand shows them.)\n- Add `--no-reset-baseline` to skip phase 0 entirely; if the crash persists,\n  it is in `resume()` proper. Either way the root-cause set is the same\n  (section 2 tables), this just tells you which sweep fired first.\n\n**Step 5 - sanity-check the pairing itself.**\n- The ELF march must fit the engine: an `-march=rv64gcv` fixture cannot run on\n  a V-less engine in *any* mode. Cross-check with a standalone cycle run:\n  `./andesim run --mode cycle --engine verilator:<cfg> <elf>`.\n- A known-good control: the same hybrid command on `verilator:ax45mpv_premium`\n  should pass end to end; that isolates the problem to the new engine's\n  config/pairing, not the harness.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Fixes\n\n**Fix 1 - regenerate the ISA sync (empty qemu.cfg).**\nIn `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim`:\n\n```sh\ngit submodule update --init external/cpu_config_to_march_converter\n./build_vsim.sh          # or re-run the cmake build for the engine target\n$VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<cfg> --print-qemu-config   # now non-empty\n```\n\nThe rebuilt binary embeds `build/V<cfg>/obj_sc/qemu.cfg`; the driver picks it\nup automatically on the next `andesim run` (no driver change needed).\n\n**Fix 2 - make the family match an instantiable QEMU model.**\nThe engine name's prefix before the first `_` must be a real Andes QEMU CPU\n(`qemu-system-riscv64 -cpu help | grep andes`), because both the qemu.cfg\n`driver=` name and (ideally) the fast leg's `-cpu` derive from it. If your\nfresh engine is not in the ax45mpv family, know that today the driver still\npins `-cpu andes-ax45mpv` for the hybrid fast leg\n(`driver/engine_registry.cpp:52-53`) - the qemu.cfg globals for another\nfamily will not apply. Options, in order of preference:\n- Framework fix: derive the fast-leg `-cpu` from the accurate engine's family\n  (same `REGEX REPLACE \"_.*$\"` rule) instead of hardcoding ax45mpv - this is\n  the real root-cause repair for non-ax45mpv engines.\n- Interim: reproduce the shell harness pairing manually - `tests/e2e.sh`\n  takes `QEMU_CPU=andes-<family>,vlen=<VLEN>` (see\n  `scripts/run_e2e.sh:123-124` for the ax66_makatau <-> andes-ax66,vlen=128\n  precedent) and runs the same two-leg flow.\n\n**Fix 3 - extension ON in the base model but absent in the engine.**\nThe converter never emits `off`, so pick a base QEMU model whose defaults are\na subset of the engine ISA (Fix 2 usually achieves this: the same-family\nmodel matches by construction), or extend\n`config_inc_to_qemu_cfg.py::_collect_isa_props` to force-off extensions the\nconfig lacks (`v`, `f`, `d` are the ones that bite: their state transport\nexecutes real instructions in the Program Buffer).\n\n**Fix 4 - memory map (RC2).**\nThe engine must map `[--shared-mem-base, base + mem_size + 4096)` as plain\nRAM in hybrid mode. Check the fresh config's `NDS_ILM_BASE` / `NDS_DLM_BASE`\ndo not fall inside the window (default window: `0x0 .. 0x08001000` for the\n128 MiB default), and `--print-memmap` shows the interconnect actually\ndecoding it. If the program needs the whole window, remember the top page is\nreserved for the transport scratch - the driver already rejects ELFs that\nreach into it, but a shrunken engine RAM window is the engine's problem to\nfix, not the driver's.\n\n**Fix 5 - caps map (only if the error is the NAMED csr_sweep variant, or a\npoll timeout/hang).**\nBind the new config.inc macro in\n`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/andes_caps_config.hpp`\n(absent macro reads as \"feature off\" - so a renamed macro silently drops the\ngate) and/or add the CSR's gate case in `andes_gate_for()`\n(`andes_caps.hpp` - an unlisted CSR defaults to ALWAYS = transported\nunconditionally). Validate offline without running anything:\n\n```sh\npython3 /home/nick/work/andesim/scripts/config_inc_to_andes_caps.py \\\n    /home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build/V<cfg>/obj_sc/config.inc\n# must equal the \"andes_csrs\" list from `sim_<cfg> --describe`\n# drift guard: tests/andes_sim/test_andes_caps_parity.sh\n```\n\n**Verify the fix.**\n\n```sh\n./andesim run --mode hybrid --engine verilator:<cfg> --trigger marker --verify <elf>\nJOBS=8 bash scripts/run_e2e.sh        # regression sweep on the stock engine\n```\n\n`--verify` matters here: it keeps the full drain and diffs the round-trip, so\nan ISA fix that merely stops the crash but drops state (e.g. V silently off\non both sides now) is caught by the state diff, and a fresh reset-baseline is\ncaptured for the new binary automatically (cache key includes the sim\nbinary's mtime/size, so no stale-baseline cleanup is needed).\n\n---\n\n## 6. Quick reference\n\nFault-to-cause map for the raw error:\n\n| first faulting PB insn | likely cause |\n|---|---|\n| `fsd`/`fld` (FPR sweep) | state FLAG_FP set, engine has no FPU -> RC1 |\n| `vsetvl`, `vle8.v`, `vse8.v` | state FLAG_V set, engine has no V -> RC1 |\n| `csrrw x0, <csr>, x5` (fallback) | abstract write NAK'd then CSR truly absent (fcsr/vstart/... on no-FP/no-V core) -> RC1 |\n| `ld`/`sd` (GPR sweep) | scratch page unmapped/blocked -> RC2 |\n\nKey files:\n\n| file | role |\n|---|---|\n| `vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp` | thrower; `exec_progbuf`, `pb_block_sweep`, `csr_sweep`, `pb_v_data_sweep` |\n| `vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/resume_driver.hpp` | resume order; `write_csr_abstract_or_pb` fallback |\n| `vsim_andesim/src/simulator.hpp` | `apply_resume_from_qemu`: baseline capture runs before resume |\n| `vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/andes_caps{,_config}.hpp` | CSR capability gates from config.inc |\n| `vsim_andesim/cmake/VerilatorHelpers.cmake` | qemu.cfg generation + family-name rule |\n| `andesim/driver/run_plan.cpp` | `--print-qemu-config` probe -> `-readconfig` |\n| `andesim/driver/engine_registry.cpp` | pinned hybrid fast-leg `-cpu andes-ax45mpv` |\n| `andesim/qemu_plugin/hybrid_handoff.c` | flags come from QEMU's advertised features, not the workload |\n"}], "grading": {"expectations": [{"text": "diagnosis attributes cmderr=3 to a Program-Buffer batch faulting - naming the CSR sweep hitting a caps over-claimed CSR (and/or mstatus.FS/VS Off for FPR/V sweeps) as the concrete cause on a new engine", "passed": true, "evidence": "Attributes cmderr=3 to an injected PB instruction trapping; That sweep walks the\n   COMPLETE caps-supported CSR list, built at compile time from the new\n   engine's `config.inc` `_NDS_*` macros (`andes_caps_from_config()`,\n   `src/hybrid/andes_caps_config.hpp`). If the gate map in\n   `src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp` (`andes_gate_for`) over-claims ONE CSR the new\n   RTL does not implement, the PB `csrr` of it raises illegal instruction ->\n   cmderr=3 -> `exec_progbuf` throws (`src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp:201-205`)\n   -> `[critical] fatal exception` (`src/main.cpp:544`). This happens at\n   resume because the capture runs inside `apply_resume_from_qemu`\n   (simulator.hpp:208-237), before any state is restored.\n\n2. **mstatus.FS/VS Off (or the extension absent) for the FPR/V sweeps.** The\n   handoff flags come from QEMU's fuller CPU model. If the state carries\n   `HYBRID_FLAG_FP`/`HYBRID_FLAG_V` but the new engine has no FPU/V at all,\n   the capture's mstatus enable write WARL-pins FS/VS to 0\n   (simulator.hpp:359-384) and the subsequent `fsd`/`vsetvl`/`vse8.v` sweep\n   instructions raise illegal instruction -> the same raw cmderr=3. The\n   restore-side mirrors (`fld`/`vle8.v` sweeps, the Tier-B `vsetvl` at\n   `resume_driver.hpp:244`, the PB `csrrw` fallback at\n   `resume_driver.hpp:452-461`) fault the same way.\n\nRarer third cause: a scratch-page access fault. Every sweep `ld/sd/fld/fsd/\nvle8/vse8` targets the top page of RAM (`v_scratch_gpa()`,\nsimulator.hpp:338-342). If the new engine's memory map/decoder does not cover\nthat page, the access raises a store/load fault -> cmderr=3 on the very first\nsweep batch, before any CSR is touched.\n\nKey background fact (from the skill's caps-gating notes): the tolerant\nabstract path MASKS over-claims -- a faulting abstract read mirrors as zero,\nQEMU drains zero too, and `--verify` falsely matches. Only the batched PB\nsweep truly validates the caps table, which is exactly why a brand-new engine\nconfig fails HERE first: the cold-cache baseline capture is the first thing\nthat runs the sweep over the new caps set.\n\n## Why you saw the raw message instead of a named CSR\n\nThe CSR sweep is self-bisecting: when a batch faults it re-runs one CSR at a\ntime and throws the actionable form\n(`src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp:318-335`):\n\n```\ncsr_sweep: CSR 0x17 raised cmderr -- the caps map over-claims a CSR the hart\ndoes not implement (fix andes_caps)\n```\n\nThe RAW `exec_progbuf: PB execution raised cmderr=3` surfaces when the\nfaulting PB execution is one the bisector does not wrap:\n\n- the GPR `sd` / FPR `fsd` block sweeps of the capture drain\n  (`pb_block_sweep`, program_buffer.hpp:258),\n- the V `vsetvl` / grouped `vse8.v`-`vle8.v` sweeps\n  (program_buffer.hpp:383,396),\n- the resume-side Tier-B `vsetvl` (resume_driver.hpp:244) or the\n  one-instruction PB `csrrw` fallback (resume_driver.hpp:460),\n- or (edge case) the bisect's re-throw when no single CSR reproduces the\n  batch fault (program_buffer.hpp:335).\n\nSo the raw message points at the FS/VS-or-extension-absent family or the\nscratch-page family; the named `csr_sweep: CSR 0x...` message points at a\ncaps over-claim. Step 1 below gets you the discriminating evidence.\n\n## Step-by-step fix\n\n### Step 1: phase-locate the fault and get the offending name\n\nRerun the exact failing command and keep the full vsim log\n(`./andesim run --mode hybrid ... 2>&1 | tee /tmp/hybrid.log`).\n\n- If the log shows `csr_sweep: CSR 0x... raised cmderr` you already have the\n  offending CSR's address. Go to Step 3.\n- If it shows only the raw `exec_progbuf` message, use the surrounding lines:\n  the info line `hybrid: reset baseline captured -- one-time cost per engine\n  build + ELF` (simulator.hpp:233-237) prints only AFTER a successful\n  capture. Error BEFORE that line = fault inside the baseline-capture drain\n  (FS/VS sweep, scratch page, or a batch the bisect could not reproduce);\n  error AFTER it = fault inside the restore proper (FPR/V reload sweeps,\n  Tier-B vsetvl, or the PB csrrw fallback).\n\n### Step 2: establish ground truth for the new engine (read-only)\n\nThe engine's compiled-in config is the ONLY truth about what it implements.\nDo NOT trust QEMU -- its gdbstub exposes CSRs the vsim RTL lacks.\n\n```sh\n# BUILD-directory copy, not the external/ source tree:\ngrep _NDS_ $VSIM_BIN_DIR/V<engine>/obj_sc/config.inc | less\n# e.g. /home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build/V<engine>/obj_sc/config.inc\n\n# What the sim binary itself claims to transport:\n$VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<engine> --describe\n```\n\nCross-check three things:\n\n- Is the fault a caps over-claim? Look up the named CSR's gate in\n  `vsim src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp` (`andes_gate_for`) and its `_NDS_*`\n  binding in `andes_caps_config.hpp`, then compare against the SPA's IM\n  Requirement (existence condition) for that CSR. The two historical bugs\n  are the template: `mcountermask_m` (0x7D1) exists when\n  `mmsc_cfg.PMNDS==1 AND misa[20]==1` but was gated PERF_MONITOR (the PMNDS\n  half only) and faulted on the M-only premium; `jvt` (0x017) is Zcmt's\n  table-jump base but was gated CODENSE and faulted on every CoDense core.\n  Rule: gate on the spec's existence condition EXACTLY; when the condition\n  needs both a feature and a privilege level, the privilege gate is the\n  binding one for current engines.\n- Is it the FS/VS family? Check the config.inc FPU/vector knobs against the\n  handoff flags (does the paired QEMU `-cpu` model F/D/V the engine lacks?).\n- Is it the scratch page? Check the engine's RAM region base/size in its\n  config/YAML against the driver's `--mem-size` (+1 page) mapping.\n\n### Step 3: fix the root cause -- no workaround\n\n**Caps over-claim (the common case).** Fix the gate in the vsim repo,\ntest-first, per the skill's TDD recipe:\n\n1. RED: in `vsim tests/hybrid/andes_caps.test.cpp`, model the NEW engine's\n   config.inc shape as a caps helper (mirror the existing `premium()`\n   helper: set exactly the gates its `_NDS_*` macros turn on). Assert the\n   expected `supports_csr()` verdicts, including\n   `supports_csr(<offender>) == false`. Build + run in the vsim container\n   and show the failing assertion:\n   ```sh\n   podman run --rm -v /home/nick/work/vsim_andesim:/work -w /work \\\n     localhost/vsim-env:latest \\\n     bash -c 'cmake --build build -j && ctest --test-dir build -LE hybrid-e2e'\n   ```\n2. GREEN: move/add the `case` in `andes_gate_for`\n   (`src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp`) so it encodes the SPA's IM Requirement, and\n   bind any new gate to its `_NDS_*` macro in `andes_caps_config.hpp`\n   (an absent macro reads as \"off\" -- never a compile error, so binding a\n   not-yet-existing knob is safe). Comment the spec condition at the case.\n3. Rebuild the sim target; unit suite green.\n\n**FS/VS / missing extension.** The capture and resume already force\nFS/VS to Initial before touching FPRs/V (simulator.hpp:359-384,\nresume_driver.hpp:172-179); if the write cannot take because the extension\ndoes not exist in the RTL, the engine cannot carry that state at all -- the\nfix is pairing: run the workload on an engine that implements what the QEMU\nleg drains (or a QEMU `-cpu` matching what the engine implements). Do not\npatch the sweep to skip faults.\n\n**Scratch page.** Fix the engine's memory configuration so the mapped RAM\nspan (including the top transport page) is decodable; this is an engine\nconfig bug, not a transport bug.\n\n### Step 4: validate with a cold-cache rerun\n\nThe baseline capture is the caps validator, so force it to re-run:\n\n```sh\nrm -rf ~/.cache/andesim   # force a fresh reset-baseline capture\n./andesim run --mode hybrid --trigger marker --engine vsim:<engine> \\\n  --verify tests/fixtures/rt_c_hello.elf\n```\n\n(A rebuilt sim binary also self-invalidates the cache -- the key includes\nits mtime+size -- but clearing the cache makes the rerun unconditional.)\n\nA clean capture empirically proves every caps-claimed CSR is csrr-able on\nthis RTL. If another CSR is over-claimed, the run aborts again with the\nbisected name -- fix that gate the same way and repeat until the capture is\nclean.\n\n**Expected and correct:** the `--verify` compared-field count may DROP by\none per de-claimed CSR (premium went 146 -> 144 when 0x7D1 and 0x017 left\nthe set). Those fields were zero-vs-zero false matches before; the drop is\nthe fix working, not a regression.\n\nThen climb the rest of the ladder:\n\n```sh\n# coverage guard (andesim repo): inventory == matrix == ABI\nbash tests/andes_csr/test_csr_coverage_complete.sh\n\n# full e2e matrix\nQEMU_BIN_DIR=... VSIM_BIN_DIR=... VSIM_QEMU=... VSIM_QEMU32=... \\\nVSIM_PLUGIN=/home/nick/work/andesim/qemu_plugin/libhybrid_handoff.so \\\nJOBS=8 bash scripts/run_e2e.sh\n```\n\n### Step 5: commit and document\n\nTidy First: the caps fix (with its test) is one behavioral commit on a green\nsuite. Update the vsim `ROADMAP.md` known-limits and andesim\n`docs/USER_GUIDE.md` section 4.4 if the verify-compared set changed; refresh\n`tests/andes_csr/csr_coverage.csv` through the classifier\n(`scripts/classify_csr_coverage.py`), never by hand.\n\n## What NOT to do\n\n- **Do not disable the CSR sweep** (`set_csr_sweep(false)`) or fall back to\n  abstract-only transport to make the run pass. The sweep is the only thing\n  that validates the caps table; the tolerant abstract path mirrors a\n  faulting read as zero and `--verify` false-matches zero==zero -- both\n  historical caps bugs were \"verified\" for months this way.\n- **Do not catch/clear the cmderr and continue.** A partially executed PB\n  batch leaves the hart state partially restored/drained, which is worse\n  than the abort (this is why `exec_progbuf` throws by design,\n  program_buffer.hpp:177-181).\n- **Do not widen caps to `AndesCaps::all()`** or gate off a whole feature to\n  dodge one CSR -- fix the one gate to the spec's existence condition.\n- **Do not turn ON the restore-side CSR sweep** while debugging: it is off\n  by design (simulator.hpp:266-272) because a `csrrw` to a read-only or\n  WARL-illegal field faults the batch with no idempotent retry story.\n- **Do not exclude the CSR in `driver/state_diff.cpp`** -- verify exclusion\n  is only for engine-reset divergences (msp_bound/msp_base, milmb/mdlmb\n  enable bit, mip timer bit), never to hide a transport/caps bug.\n\n## Appendix: cmderr decode and fault-site map\n\n`abstractcs.cmderr` (Debug Spec): 1=busy, 2=not supported, **3=exception\nduring command**, 4=halt/resume, 5=bus error, 7=other. Code 3 always means\nan instruction the DM executed on the hart trapped.\n\nPB executions that can raise the raw message at resume, in run order on a\ncold-cache hybrid run:\n\n| Phase | PB content | Typical trap cause |\n|-------|-----------|--------------------|\n| baseline capture drain | GPR `sd` sweep | scratch page unmapped |\n| baseline capture drain | FPR `fsd` sweep | no FPU / FS pinned Off |\n| baseline capture drain | `vsetvl` + `vse8.v` dump sweep | no V / VS pinned Off |\n| baseline capture drain | `csrr`+`sd` CSR sweep | caps over-claim (bisected -> named error) |\n| restore | `vsetvl` + `vle8.v` reload sweep | no V / VS pinned Off |\n| restore | Tier-B `vsetvl x0,x5,x6` | no V |\n| restore | FPR `fld` sweep, GPR `ld` sweep | no FPU / scratch page |\n| restore | PB `csrrw` fallback (mstatus, fcsr, vstart/vxsat/vxrm/vcsr, dcsr, mcycle, minstret) | CSR absent on this RTL |\n\nThe exit-drain runs the same sweeps again; a fault there (after the ROI)\nhas the same triage, but \"dies at resume\" on a fresh engine means the\nbaseline capture or the restore above.\n"}], "grading": {"expectations": [{"text": "diagnosis attributes cmderr=3 to a Program-Buffer batch faulting - naming the CSR sweep hitting a caps over-claimed CSR (and/or mstatus.FS/VS Off for FPR/V sweeps) as the concrete cause on a new engine", "passed": true, "evidence": "'A Program-Buffer batch faulted on the halted hart'; cause 1: 'Caps over-claim hit by the CSR sweep during the cold-cache reset-baseline capture ... the PB csrr of it raises illegal instruction -> cmderr=3'; cause 2: 'mstatus.FS/VS Off (or the extension absent) for the FPR/V sweeps'. The error TEXT is the discriminator\n(skill: `references/validation.md`, rung 3): the batched CSR sweep bisects its\nfaults and rethrows a RENAMED error (`csr_sweep: CSR 0x... raised cmderr --\nthe caps map over-claims ...`), so a RAW `exec_progbuf: ...` means the fault\nis in one of the NON-CSR PB sites. On a freshly configured engine the two\nrealistic causes are, in order of likelihood:\n\n1. **FP/V flag over-claim from a broken QEMU-side ISA pairing.** The QEMU\n   fast leg ran a `-cpu` that models F/D and/or V, so the plugin drained\n   `HYBRID_FLAG_FP` / `HYBRID_FLAG_V`, but the new RTL config has no FPU/VPU.\n   The engine WARL-pins `mstatus.FS/VS` to Off, and the first `fld`/`fsd` /\n   `vsetvl`/`vle8.v`/`vse8.v` in a PB raises illegal instruction ->\n   cmderr=3. Root cause: the hw-config sync chain (engine `config.inc` ->\n   baked `qemu.cfg` -> `--print-qemu-config` -> QEMU `-readconfig`) is broken\n   for the fresh engine, so the driver fell back to its default full-featured\n   `-cpu` (`andes-ax46mpv`).\n2. **Scratch-page access fault.** Every PB sweep rides the mmap TOP page\n   (`v_scratch_gpa = ram_base + mem_size`). If the fresh engine's memory map\n   does not decode that page, the very first sweep (`sd` GPR block-store in\n   the baseline capture -- runs even on scalar-only handoffs) raises an\n   access fault -> cmderr=3.\n\nA third, less likely cause: the caps map over-claims a CSR AND the sim binary\npredates the bisect feature (or the batch fault does not reproduce\nsingle-CSR), so the error stays raw instead of being renamed.\n\n## Why this hits exactly at \"resume\" on a FIRST run\n\nThe resume leg (`vsim_andesim/src/simulator.hpp:apply_resume_from_qemu`,\nlines 208-286) does, in order:\n\n1. **Reset-baseline capture** (cold cache -- always cold on a freshly built\n   engine, the cache key includes the sim binary's mtime+size):\n   `capture_reset_baseline` (line 349) halts the freshly reset hart, enables\n   `mstatus.FS/VS` if the incoming flags claim FP/V, then runs a FULL drain\n   with all sweeps on:\n   - GPR `sd` block-store sweep (first PB user; `state_drain.hpp` line 95)\n   - FPR `fsd` sweep (line 109)\n   - Andes+M-ext `csrr+sd` CSR sweep (line 225) -- faults here get RENAMED\n   - V `vse8.v` dump sweep LAST (line 263)\n2. **`ResumeDriver::resume()`** (`resume_driver.hpp` line 146): mstatus\n   FS/VS enable -> FPR `fld` sweep -> V `vle8.v` sweep + `vsetvl` PB ->\n   priv/PMP -> Andes/M-ext CSRs (abstract, tolerant; restore-side CSR sweep\n   is OFF by design) -> GPR `ld` sweep -> counters.\n\nSo on a fresh engine the fault usually fires inside the baseline capture or\nthe first sweep of `resume()` -- both are \"at resume\" from the user's view,\nand both throw out to `main.cpp:544` (`[critical] fatal exception: ...`).\n\nKey asymmetry that tells cause 1 from cause 2:\n- FS/VS-off illegal instruction can only fire in the FPR/V sweeps, and only\n  when the handoff flags carry FP/V.\n- A scratch-page access fault fires in EVERY sweep, including the GPR `sd`\n  sweep of a scalar-only handoff.\n\n## Step-by-step triage (read-only, minutes)\n\n1. **Confirm the error is raw.** If the message starts `csr_sweep: CSR 0x...`\n   it is a caps over-claim -- skip to Fix C and the standard caps flow. Raw\n   `exec_progbuf: ...` -> continue.\n\n2. **Confirm the binary has the bisect** (rules out a silent caps\n   over-claim on an old binary):\n   ```sh\n   strings $VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<engine> | grep -c \"csr_sweep: CSR\"\n   ```\n   `0` -> the binary predates the bisect; a raw cmderr=3 can still be the\n   caps map. Rebuild from current source before further triage.\n\n3. **Read the drained flags** from the handoff file the QEMU leg produced\n   (the driver's workdir `state-0.bin`, or rerun QEMU1 only). `flags` is a\n   little-endian u32 at byte offset 8 of `hybrid_state_v1`:\n   ```sh\n   od -An -tx4 -j8 -N4 state-0.bin\n   ```\n   bit0=RV64, bit1=FP, bit2=V, bit3=ANDES, bit4=M_EXT.\n\n4. **Get the engine's ground truth** (skill Phase 0: the BUILD-dir\n   config.inc is the only truth; never trust QEMU):\n   ```sh\n   grep -E \"NDS_FPU_TYPE|NDS_VLEN|NDS_NUM_PRIVILEGE_LEVELS\" \\\n     <vsim>/build/V<engine>/obj_sc/config.inc\n   $VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<engine> --describe\n   ```\n\n5. **Compare.** Flags claim FP but config.inc has no FPU, or flags claim V\n   but no `NDS_VLEN`/VPU -> **Cause 1 confirmed.** Then check where the\n   pairing chain broke:\n   ```sh\n   $VSIM_BIN_DIR/sim_<engine> --print-qemu-config\n   ```\n   - Empty output -> the engine was built with an empty `qemu.cfg` blob. The\n     build log says `No CPU_CONFIG_QEMU_CONVERTER; embedding empty qemu.cfg`\n     (`vsim_andesim/cmake/VerilatorHelpers.cmake:157-163`) -- typically the\n     `external/cpu_config_to_march_converter` submodule was never\n     initialized in the fresh checkout. With an empty probe the andesim\n     driver keeps the fast leg on its DEFAULT `-cpu andes-ax46mpv`\n     (`driver/engine_registry.cpp:65`), which models full FP+V.\n   - Non-empty but wrong (stale ISA, wrong vlen) -> the cfg was generated\n     from a different config.inc; the engine build dir is out of date.\n\n6. **If flags match the engine's caps**, check the scratch page\n   (**Cause 2**): the driver maps `--mem-size` plus one page; every sweep\n   targets `ram_base + mem_size .. +4K`. Verify the fresh engine's platform\n   memory region (its config/YAML, `platform.main_memory.region`) covers\n   that page for the `--mem-size` used. A region sized exactly to a smaller\n   RAM, or a different base address, faults every PB load/store. Cheap\n   cross-check without rebuilds: a scalar fixture (no FP/V flags) faulting\n   the same way points at the scratch page, not FS/VS.\n\n7. **Locate capture vs resume** (refines the diagnosis, optional): if the\n   log shows `hybrid: reset baseline captured ...` BEFORE the fatal, the\n   capture succeeded and the fault is in `resume()`; if the fatal comes\n   first, it died inside the capture drain. `--no-reset-baseline` skips the\n   capture entirely (the same FPR/V sweeps still run at resume, so a\n   Cause-1/2 fault only moves, it does not disappear -- itself a useful\n   signal that this is not a baseline-cache artifact).\n\n## The fix\n\n### Cause 1: broken QEMU pairing (most likely on a fresh engine)\n\n1. In the vsim checkout, initialize the converter and rebuild the engine so\n   `qemu.cfg` is baked from THIS config.inc:\n   ```sh\n   git submodule update --init external/cpu_config_to_march_converter\n   ./build_vsim.sh    # container build; look for\n                      # \"Generating qemu.cfg for <engine> (andes-<family>)\"\n   ```\n   (The converter path is the `CPU_CONFIG_QEMU_CONVERTER` cmake cache var,\n   `VerilatorHelpers.cmake:10-12`; the emitted driver name must match\n   `-cpu andes-<family>`, family = engine name minus the config suffix.)\n2. Verify: `sim_<engine> --print-qemu-config` now emits a cfg whose ISA\n   matches config.inc (no `f`/`d` without an FPU, no `v` without a VPU,\n   vlen == `NDS_VLEN`).\n3. Cold-cache rerun (the rebuild self-invalidates the cache anyway, but be\n   explicit):\n   ```sh\n   rm -rf ~/.cache/andesim\n   ./andesim run --mode hybrid --trigger marker --engine vsim:<engine> \\\n     --verify tests/fixtures/rt_c_hello.elf\n   ```\n   The fast leg now runs `-readconfig` with the engine-matched ISA, the\n   plugin's gdbstub handles for F/V never bind, the flags stay clear, and\n   no FPR/V sweep is issued.\n4. Interim workaround only (no rebuild possible): pass an engine-matched\n   `-cpu` through the post-`--` QEMU args. Do not keep this: the baked\n   `qemu.cfg` is the single source of truth and survives the next user.\n\n### Cause 2: scratch page not decoded\n\nFix the fresh engine's platform memory region so it covers\n`ram_base .. ram_base + mem_size + 4K` for the intended `--mem-size`, and\nrebuild; or run with a `--mem-size` small enough that the top page falls\ninside the engine's decoded RAM. There is no correct workaround that skips\nthe scratch page: GPR/FPR/CSR sweeps fall back to abstract commands only\nwhen `*_scratch_fits` fails (a vlenb-driven condition), not on decode\nerrors, and a V handoff requires the page.\n\n### Cause 3: caps over-claim (old binary / bisect did not reproduce)\n\nRebuild from current source so the bisect names the CSR, then follow the\nstandard caps flow (skill Phase 3 + `references/caps-gating.md`):\n1. RED: model the fresh engine's config.inc shape as a helper in\n   `tests/hybrid/andes_caps.test.cpp` (set exactly the gates its `_NDS_*`\n   macros turn on) and assert the failing `supports_csr()` verdict.\n2. GREEN: fix the `case` in `andes_gate_for` (`src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp`)\n   and, if the gate is new, its `_NDS_*` binding in\n   `src/hybrid/andes_caps_config.hpp`. Gate on the SPEC's existence\n   condition exactly, not a correlated feature.\n3. Rebuild, cold-cache rerun; repeat until the capture is clean. Expect the\n   `--verify` field count to DROP by one per de-claimed CSR (correct: those\n   were zero-vs-zero false matches).\n\n## After the fix: climb the ladder, then register the engine\n\nValidation ladder (`references/validation.md`), bottom-up:\n1. vsim unit suite (`ctest -LE hybrid-e2e` in the container),\n2. andesim coverage guard (`bash tests/andes_csr/test_csr_coverage_complete.sh`),\n3. cold-cache hybrid run with `--verify` (the real caps validator),\n4. `--verify` field-diff triage (reset-divergent fields -> named exclusions\n   in `driver/state_diff.cpp`, never to hide transport bugs),\n5. full e2e matrix (`JOBS=8 bash scripts/run_e2e.sh`).\n\nA freshly configured engine is a NEW registration by definition (identity =\nengine name + config.inc hash, `references/engine-registration.md`): produce\nits CSR manifest, both-direction completeness check, generated\nwrite-survival fixtures, and the registration guard entry under\n`tests/engines/<engine>-<confighash8>/`. Keep a cold-cache hybrid run in the\nengine's registered test set so this failure class stays a TEST, not an\nincident.\n\n## Evidence trail (files read)\n\n- `andesim/.claude/skills/csr-transport-bringup/SKILL.md` + references\n  (validation.md rung 3 carries the raw-vs-renamed discriminator).\n- `vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/program_buffer.hpp:183-206` (raw throw site),\n  `289-341` (csr_sweep bisect + rename; The identity: an engine build = (core, config)\n\nThe engine's compiled-in config is the only truth about which CSRs it\nimplements. The registration key is the engine name plus a short hash\n(first 8 hex chars) of the BUILD-directory config:\n\n```\n$VSIM_BIN_DIR/V<engine>/obj_sc/config.inc\n```\n\nConcretely, with today's `config.env` (`VSIM_BIN_DIR=/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build`):\n\n- `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build/Vax45mpv_premium/obj_sc/config.inc`\n- `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build/Vax46mpv_advanced/obj_sc/config.inc`\n\nUse the build copy, never the `external/` source tree (it can differ from\nwhat was actually built), and never QEMU's view (its gdbstub advertises CSRs\nthe vsim RTL does not implement; sweeping one stalls or faults the Debug\nModule).\n\nArtifact layout in the andesim repo:\n\n```\ntests/engines/<engine>-<confighash8>/\n|-- engine.env          # engine name, QEMU -cpu pairing, march, vlen, priv levels\n|-- csr_manifest.csv    # deliverable 1\n`-- fixtures/           # deliverable 3 (generated)\n```\n\nRebuilding the same core with a different config changes the hash, which\nchanges the directory name, which makes the guard (deliverable 4) refuse to\nrun the engine against the old artifacts. \"Same core, new config\" cannot be\nsilently reused -- reuse is impossible by construction, not by review\ndiscipline.\n\n## 2. Deliverable 1: the CSR manifest (`csr_manifest.csv`)\n\nOne row per spec-inventory CSR -- ALL of them (the current inventory,\n`tests/andes_csr/csr_inventory.csv`, has 232 CSRs from the SPA), not just\nthe transported ones:\n\n```\naddr,name,access,exists_cond,implemented,transported,gate_or_disposition,reset_value\n0x7D1,mcountermask_m,MRW,PMNDS&misa[20],no,no,UMODE(off),-\n0x7CF,mcounterinten,MRW,PMNDS,yes,yes,PERF_MONITOR,0x0\n```\n\nEach column comes from a DIFFERENT source, and they must agree:\n\n- `exists_cond`: the SPA spec (per-CSR IM Requirement / Table 13) --\n  Phase 1 output.\n- `implemented`: EMPIRICAL, from the probe (section 3) -- never inferred\n  from QEMU.\n- `transported`: the caps verdict of THIS binary --\n  `sim_<engine> --describe` emits the `andes_csrs` JSON list the engine\n  compiled in (`src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp` + `andes_caps_config.hpp` in the\n  vsim repo).\n- `reset_value`: EMPIRICAL -- from the probe or the reset-baseline capture\n  (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME/andesim/*.bin` is a `hybrid_state_v1` struct; parse with\n  the ABI header `include/hybrid/state_abi.h`). This IS the engine's reset\n  value table: generated, reviewed once by a human against the spec's reset\n  column (IM/DC fields will differ from the spec -- expected and fine), then\n  committed.\n\n## 3. Deliverable 2: the both-direction completeness check\n\n\"Nothing missed\" has two failure directions; each needs its own check.\n\n**Over-claim** (caps says yes, RTL says no): the batched Program-Buffer CSR\nsweep faults (cmderr=3) on a single over-claimed CSR and bisects to name it.\nThis matters because the tolerant abstract-command path MASKS this bug: a\nfaulting read mirrors as zero, QEMU drains zero too, and `--verify` falsely\nmatches. A cold-cache hybrid run exercises the sweep via the reset-baseline\ncapture -- keep one in the engine's registered test set so an over-claim is\na TEST failure, not a field incident.\n\n**Under-claim** (RTL implements it, caps gates it off -- the silent\nstate-loss case): script it from the manifest. Every row whose\n`exists_cond` evaluates true against this `config.inc` (or whose probe says\n`implemented == yes`) must have `transported == yes` OR a named `EXCL_*`\ndisposition from the coverage matrix (`tests/andes_csr/csr_coverage.csv`).\nA row with neither FAILS the script. No human list-checking.\n\n**The probe** that feeds both: a checked read of every inventory CSR on the\nhalted, freshly reset hart. The DM mirrors an unimplemented CSR as a\ncmderr, which `read_register_64_checked` reports as nullopt -- so one pass\n(~232 reads, seconds of sim time) yields the implemented set AND the\nempirical reset values. EXCL_SIDEFX rows (reads with side effects) are\nskipped by policy. The sim binaries have no probe mode today (grep of\n`src/hybrid/` and `main.cpp` in the vsim repo finds none); building it is\npart of the FIRST registration -- a small loop over the inventory using the\nexisting DM helpers, done test-first.\n\n## 4. Deliverable 3: generated per-engine write-survival fixtures\n\nWrite-survival tests must come from a GENERATOR keyed by the manifest, not\nfrom hand-written files. Today's CSR fixtures (`tests/fixtures/\nrt_csr_survival.S`, `rt_m_ext_counters.S`, `rt_m_ext_trigcause.S`) are\nhand-written and engine-generic; hand-written sets rot and miss CSRs when a\nnew engine claims a different set.\n\nFor each transported read/write CSR the generator emits: a distinctive\nwrite BEFORE the ROI (proves resume restores it into the vsim hart) and one\nINSIDE the ROI (proves drain captures it), asserted via the drained state\nand `--verify`.\n\nThe generator needs a per-CSR safe-write policy, because blind writes to\ncontrol CSRs break the run (`mcache_ctl` flips caches, `milmb`/`mdlmb`\nmove local memory, `mhsp_ctl` arms stack protection). Three tiers:\n\n- plain state (scratch-like, masks, event selects): full distinctive pattern;\n- WARL-constrained: write within a reviewed mask;\n- dangerous control CSRs: skip-list with a comment saying why -- their\n  round-trip is still covered by `--verify` on real workload values.\n\nThe policy table is reviewed once and committed next to the generator.\n\n## 5. Deliverable 4: the registration guard\n\nA script that cross-checks: every engine that `./andesim list engines`\nreports (driver F2 discovery, `driver/cmd_list.cpp:list_engines`) must have\na `tests/engines/<engine>-<confighash8>/` directory whose hash matches the\nCURRENT binary's `config.inc`, with the manifest and generated fixtures\npresent. An unregistered engine, or one whose hash went stale after a\nrebuild, fails with \"run the bring-up registration\". Wire it next to the\nexisting coverage guard (`tests/andes_csr/check_coverage.py` /\n`test_csr_coverage_complete.sh`) so CI runs both.\n\n## 6. How the checks compose (\"nothing missed\" as machine invariants)\n\nLayered guards, each catching what the others cannot:\n\n1. `tests/andes_csr/check_coverage.py` (exists today): inventory == matrix\n   == ABI (`state_abi.h` X-macros `HYBRID_ANDES_CSR_LIST` /\n   `HYBRID_M_EXT_CSR_LIST`). A CSR added to the wire format without a\n   disposition, or vice versa, fails loudly. Engine-independent.\n2. `tests/andes_sim/test_andes_caps_parity.sh` +\n   `scripts/config_inc_to_andes_caps.py` (exist today): the offline Python\n   gate logic run on the engine's build `config.inc` must reproduce the\n   built binary's `--describe andes_csrs` list -- offline config == compiled\n   caps.\n3. The under-claim script (deliverable 2, per engine): implemented-but-not-\n   transported-and-not-excluded fails.\n4. The cold-cache sweep run (deliverable 2, per engine): over-claims fault\n   and name the CSR.\n5. The registration guard (deliverable 4): unregistered or stale-hash\n   engines cannot run at all.\n6. Named verify exclusions live in ONE place\n   (`driver/state_diff.cpp:andes_verify_excluded`, currently e.g.\n   `msp_bound`/`msp_base`, `milmb`/`mdlmb`, `mip`), each with a comment\n   naming the engine-reset divergence. Exclusion is only for\n   engine-implementation artifacts, never to hide a transport bug.\n\nRebuild-with-different-config is covered by check 5: the new `config.inc`\nhashes differently, the old `tests/engines/` dir no longer matches, and\nevery engine-scoped artifact (manifest, probe results, fixtures) must be\nregenerated and re-reviewed under the new key.\n\n## 7. Matrix runner integration\n\nThe full ~47-fixture suite stays on the default engines (wall-time). Correct to keep it in the wire format for U-mode-capable engines (e.g. ax66_makatau). Historical note: this was a real over-claim bug -- it was wrongly gated `PERF_MONITOR` and was fixed to `UMODE` on 2026-07-02 when the drain CSR sweep exposed the fault.\n\n## 3. tcontrol (0x7A5)\n\n**Spec existence requirement**: part of the Trigger Module, and OPTIONAL even when triggers exist.\n- Section 6.6.6 \"Trigger Control Register\", printed page 140 (PDF page 154): \"IM Requirement: Trigger Module optional\". The section explains it is one of two allowed solutions for M-mode triggers firing inside M-mode trap handlers (MTE/MPTE), recommended for M-only systems.\n- Table 13 does not list tcontrol; trigger existence is probed via the tselect loop (printed page 34, PDF page 47). So spec-wise \"trigger module present\" does not guarantee tcontrol -- it must be checked per implementation.\n\n**Access mode**: MRW per Table 5 \"RISC-V Standard Machine Mode CSRs\", printed page 21 (PDF page 35). Section 6.6.6: \"Access Mode: Machine and Debug\", \"CSR Address: 0x7A5 (standard read/write)\". Writable fields: MTE bit [3] and MPTE bit [7], both RW, reset 0 (printed page 141, PDF page 155).\n\n**Premium implements it: YES.**\n- RTL decoder: `(csr_addr == CSR12_TCONTROL)` appears UNCONDITIONALLY in `existent_csr` (`kv_csr_dec.v` line 471; `CSR12_TCONTROL = 12'h7a5`, line 331) -- this core family always implements tcontrol when built, independent of trigger count. tcontrol logic also present in `ucore/hdl/kv_trigger.v`, `kv_trigm.v`, `kv_csr.v`.\n- The engine has a trigger module: `NDS_NUM_TRIGGER 2` (config.inc line 374).\n\n**Should transport: YES**, and the current code already does. MTE/MPTE are plain RW M-mode state; in a trap-free, no-debug-trigger ROI they are inert but must round-trip so the resumed hart matches the drained one. In the v4 wire list (`state_abi.h` line 165), gated `TRIGGER` (`andes_caps.hpp` lines 105-106, together with tselect/tdata1/tdata2/mcontext), bound to `_NDS_NUM_TRIGGER > 0` (`andes_caps_config.hpp` lines 66-68); premium defines `_NDS_NUM_TRIGGER 2` (`Vax45mpv_premium_config.h` line 646), gate ON. It is in premium's verified round-trip set. One caveat: the `TRIGGER` gate assumes trigger-module implies tcontrol, which the standard calls optional -- true for this RTL family (unconditional decode), but worth re-checking for a non-kv-core engine.\n\n## 4. jvt (0x017)\n\n**Spec existence requirement**: exists only when the Zcmt (table-jump) extension is implemented.\n- Table 10 \"RISC-V Standard User Mode CSRs\", printed page 29 (PDF page 43): listed under the \"ZCE CSRs\" group -- `0x017 URW JVT \"Table jump base vector and control register\"`. The SPA has no per-CSR detail section for it (it is defined by the RISC-V Zc spec: jvt exists iff Zcmt).\n- Zcmt presence is advertised by `mrvarch_cfg.Zcmt` (bit [31]): \"Indicates if the Zcmt extension is implemented or not\" -- printed page 217 (PDF page 230).\n- Important: Zcmt is NOT implied by Andes CoDense (exec.it + uitb). Premium has CoDense but that does not bring jvt.\n\n**Access mode**: URW (user-level read/write) per Table 10 -- unlike the other three, it is a U-level CSR address, readable/writable from any privilege level that implements it.\n\n**Premium implements it: NO.**\n- RTL: zero occurrences of `jvt` or address `12'h017` in `kv_csr_dec.v` and `kv_csr.v` (grep count 0 in both) -- the address is not even decoded, so a `csrr` raises illegal instruction.\n- Config: no `NDS_ZCMT*` macro exists in `config.inc` or `Vax45mpv_premium_config.h` (only `NDS_ZCMLSD_SUPPORT 0`, config.inc line 126, which is a different Zc knob). CoDense (`NDS_CODENSE_SUPPORT \"yes\"`, line 193) only gates uitb (0x800).\n- Empirical confirmation in `andes_caps.hpp` lines 65-68: \"a CoDense core without Zcmt faults a csrr of it (seen on ax45mpv_premium, where it aborted the drain CSR sweep)\".\n\n**Should transport: NO on this engine**, and the current code already excludes it: in the v3 wire list (`state_abi.h` line 92) but gated `ZCMT` (`andes_caps.hpp` line 68); the `ZCMT` gate is bound to `_NDS_ZCMT_SUPPORT_INT` which no current config generation emits, so the gate is off on every engine today (`andes_caps_config.hpp` lines 26-31 -- deliberately future-proofed). Like mcountermask_m, this was the second over-claim fixed on 2026-07-02 (was wrongly gated `CODENSE`).\n\n## Cross-cutting notes\n\n- The engine truth is the vsim RTL + its generated config, NOT what QEMU exposes: QEMU `andes-ax45mpv` models full M/S/U and advertises CSRs (including mcountermask_m) that this vsim build does not decode. Restoring a CSR the RTL lacks faults the Program Buffer sweep (`cmderr=3`) or, on the abstract path, silently reads zero and can false-pass `--verify`. Capability must come from `andes_caps_from_config()` over the engine's own `_NDS_*` macros.\n- Verdict for this audit: all four CSRs are already classified correctly in the current transport code. No change needed.\n\n## Evidence index\n\nSpec (all in `AndeStar_V5_SPA_UM164_V1.7.pdf`):\n- Table 5 (std M-mode CSRs, tcontrol MRW): printed p.21 / PDF p.35\n- Table 6 (Andes M-mode CSRs, 0x7CF + 0x7D1 MRW): printed p.24 / PDF p.38\n- Table 10 (std U-mode CSRs, JVT URW under \"ZCE CSRs\"): printed p.29 / PDF p.43\n- Table 13 (CSR existence summary): printed p.32 / PDF p.45\n- Section 6.6.6 tcontrol: printed p.140-142 / PDF p.154-155\n- Section 9.6.2 mcountermask_m: printed p.325 / PDF p.339\n- Section 9.6.5 mcounterinten: printed p.328 / PDF p.342\n- mrvarch_cfg.Zcmt bit: printed p.217 / PDF p.230\n\nEngine (`/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/build/Vax45mpv_premium/`):\n- `obj_sc/config.inc`: lines 103, 126, 161, 176-177, 193, 268, 374\n- `obj_sc/Vax45mpv_premium_config.h`: lines 644, 646, 652-654, 754\n- `andes_ip/kv_core/ucore/hdl/kv_csr_dec.v`: line 471 (`existent_csr`), line 82 (`s2`), localparams lines 331, 366, 367\n- `andes_ip/kv_core/top/hdl/ax45mpv_core_top.v`: lines 352, 387\n\nTransport code:\n- `/home/nick/work/andesim/include/hybrid/state_abi.h`: lines 90, 92 (v3 list), 133, 165 (v4 list)\n- `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/andes_caps.hpp`: lines 65-68, 77-84, 89-90, 105-106\n- `/home/nick/work/vsim_andesim/src/hybrid/andes_caps_config.hpp`: lines 26-31, 38-40, 60-62, 66-68\n"}], "eval-1-new-csr-disposition-with_skill-run-1": [{"name": "plan.md", "type": "text", "content": "# Plan: carrying scountermask_s in the hybrid handoff\n\n## Answer\n\nNothing needs to ride the wire: `scountermask_s` (0x9D2) is an architectural\nALIAS of `mcountermask_s` (0x7D2), and the handoff already transports\n`mcountermask_s` in the v4 M-ext block, SMODE-gated. What the SPA says (Phase 1, spec mining)\n\nSource: `AndeStar_V5_SPA_UM164_V1.7.pdf`, section 12.3.2 \"Supervisor Counter\nMask for S-Mode\" (doc page 412).\n\n| Property | Value |\n|---|---|\n| Mnemonic | `scountermask_s` |\n| CSR address | 0x9D2 (non-standard read/write) |\n| Access Mode | **Supervisor** |\n| IM Requirement (existence) | `mmsc_cfg.PMNDS==1 & misa[18]==1` (Andes perf-monitor feature AND S-mode implemented) |\n| Reset / default | 0 |\n| Aliasing | \"This register is an alias of the mcountermask_s CSR\" (12.3.2); confirmed by Table 14 \"Summary of Aliased CSRs\": `mcountermask_s, scountermask_s` |\n| Writability | Each bit is writable only if the matching bit in `mcounterwen` (0x7CE) is 1; writes to read-only bits are silently dropped (no exception). Always readable regardless of `mcounteren`. |\n| Function | A set CY/IR/HPM3~6 bit stops that counter from incrementing while in S-mode |\n\nTable 13 cross-check (consolidated existence table): `mcountermask_s`,\n`scountermask_m/s/u`, `scounterinten`, `scounterovf`, `scountinhibit`,\n`shpmevent3~6` all share the condition\n`(mmsc_cfg.PMNDS==1) & (misa[18]==1) // supervisor`.\n\nSection 9.6.1 (`mcounterwen`) explicitly lists `scountermask_s` among the\nregisters whose write behavior `mcounterwen` controls -- so the existing\nrestore-ordering prerequisite (write-enable before the masks) applies to the\nalias exactly as it does to 0x7D2.\n\n## 2. Per the SPA it is\nan architectural ALIAS of mcountermask_s (0x7D2) -- and the v4 M-ext transport\nALREADY carries mcountermask_s, SMODE-gated. Both QEMU and the Andes RTL back\nthe two addresses with one register, so the architectural state already rides\nthe handoff on S-capable engines (ax66_makatau). Recommendation: do NOT add a\nnew wire field (Option A). The real work is a validation fixture plus coverage\nmatrix/doc bookkeeping. Option B (explicit alias transport) is enumerated below\nin case the drained record must show the 0x9D2 view separately; it buys nothing\narchitecturally.\n\n## 1. What the AndeStar V5 SPA says (UM164 V1.7)\n\nSource: /home/nick/work/andesim/AndeStar_V5_SPA_UM164_V1.7.pdf\n\n- Identity: `scountermask_s`, CSR address 0x9D2, \"Supervisor Counter Mask for\n  S-Mode\" (Section 12.3.2, page 412; listed in Table 9 \"AndeStar V5 Supervisor\n  Mode CSRs\", Section 5.6, and Table 69 \"Counter Related CSRs\", Section 12.3).\n- Mode: **Access Mode: Supervisor**. Table 9 privilege column: `SRO/SRW`\n  (\"non-standard read/write\" -- per-bit writability, see below).\n- Existence (IM Requirement, Section 12.3.2 and Table 13):\n  **`mmsc_cfg.PMNDS==1 & misa[18]==1`** -- the Andes performance-monitor\n  extension must be configured AND Supervisor mode implemented (misa bit 18 =\n  'S'). Same condition as its M-mode primary mcountermask_s (Section 9.6.3) and\n  its siblings scountermask_m (0x9D1) / scountermask_u (0x9D3).\n- **Alias**: \"This register is an alias of the mcountermask_s CSR\"\n  (Section 12.3.2). Table 14 \"Summary of Aliased CSRs\" (Section 5.11) lists the\n  pair `mcountermask_s, scountermask_s`. One storage, two addresses.\n- Writability: each bit is read-only when the corresponding bit in\n  `mcounterwen` (0x7CE, Section 9.6.1) is 0; writes to read-only bits do not\n  trap. Not controlled by `mcounteren`; always readable in S-mode. The M-mode\n  primary 0x7D2 carries no such caveat (plain M-mode read/write).\n- Function/fields: bits CY(0), IR(2), HPM3..6(3..6); bits 7..31 read as 0. A\n  set bit stops that counter from incrementing **in S-mode**. Reset value 0.\n  Note: this makes it cycle-figure-relevant for an S-mode ROI -- a lost mask\n  changes whether mcycle counts during the window (same \"cycle hygiene\" reason\n  mcountermask_m is transported, per docs/andes_csr_transport_ROADMAP.md).\n\n## 2. Where the state already is today\n\n- Wire format: `mcountermask_s` (0x7D2) is already a v4 M-ext field --\n  /home/nick/work/andesim/include/hybrid/state_abi.h:134,\n  `X(mcountermask_s, 0x7D2, \"mcountermask_s\")` in `HYBRID_M_EXT_CSR_LIST`\n  (51 CSRs, `HYBRID_M_EXT_CSR_NUM`). Unrebuilt engines still accept the file\n  (same size, same version) but ignore the new field.\n\n## 5. Behavioral gotchas (apply to both options)\n\na) **mcounterwen write-gating**: S-alias bits are read-only where the matching\n   `mcounterwen` bit is 0. Any fixture writing through 0x9D2 must set\n   `mcounterwen` first or the write silently drops. The transport itself is\n   safe: it restores through the M-primary 0x7D2 (plain M-writable), and the\n   X-macro order already restores `mcounterwen` (0x7CE, list position 13)\n   before the masks (position 15).\nb) **QEMU model divergence**: QEMU's `write_scounter` writes the FULL masked\n   value when `mcounterwen == 0` and applies per-bit gating only when\n   `wen != 0` -- inverted from the spec's read-only-when-0 rule. A fixture\n   that writes 0x9D2 with wen=0 would take effect in QEMU but not in the RTL,\n   producing a false `--verify` diff. Designing the fixture to set wen bits\n   first (gotcha (a)) sidesteps the divergence.\nc) **Engine scope**: on ax45mpv_premium (M-only) the CSR does not exist; the\n   SMODE gate keeps it out of restore/drain/verify -- do not \"test\" this CSR\n   there. QEMU-exposes != vsim-implements (the known premium gotcha).\nd) **Measurement semantics**: if a future S-mode ROI runs with\n   scountermask_s.CY set, mcycle stops counting in S-mode -- the transported\n   mask value directly shapes the cycle figure. Worth a line in USER_GUIDE\n   when S-mode ROIs are documented.\n\n## 6. Suggested sequence (TDD, Option A)\n\n1.
